The key insight is that `start_frame_number` should equal `region@.start / spec_page_size()` in the spec. For `region.size()`, it simply returns the `usize` value that corresponds to `region@.size`.

Now I'm working through the frame calculations: `num_frames` is `region.size() / mem::FRAME_SIZE`, which gives us `region@.size / page_size`. Then `end_frame_number` becomes `start_frame_number + num_frames`. But I need to verify this matches the spec's calculation of `(region@.start + region@.size) / spec_page_size()`. These two approaches are equivalent only if the division distributes properly—that is, if `region@.start` is page-aligned, which it should be.

Looking at the `region.inv()` invariant, it guarantees both `region@.start` and `region@.size` are divisible by `spec_page_size()`, so the arithmetic checks out. I'll need to find or write a Verus lemma to prove the integer division equivalence formally.

Now I'm tracing through the deref chain: `region.start()` returns a `PageAligned<T>`, and then I need to see how `into_frame_number()` works on that type.

Since `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>` implements `Deref<Target = PhysicalAddress>`, the method call auto-derefs to get a `&PhysicalAddress`. The `into_frame_number` method takes `self` as an owned value, but since `PhysicalAddress` is `Copy`, Rust (and hopefully Verus) will handle the implicit copy automatically.

I should verify whether Verus properly handles auto-deref and implicit copying for `Copy` types in method calls, but assuming it does, this should work. The real challenge is managing all these type conversions and ensuring the proof obligations are satisfied at each step. I need to trace through how `Deref` works here—`PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>` dereferences to `PhysicalAddress` through an external body, and then `into_frame_number` operates on that dereferenced value, so the view should carry through from the original `PageAligned` wrapper to produce the frame number.

Now I'm thinking about what specifications to add to `region.rs`, particularly for `TruncatedMemoryRegion::start()`. I need to specify that the returned `PageAligned<T>` has a view matching the region's start field, and that the invariant is preserved. The tricky part is handling the generic `T` and making sure the view specification works across all monomorphizations.

For the `size()` method, I'm adding a similar ensures clause that converts the returned `usize` to `int` and compares it against the region's size field. These are minimal, non-invasive changes that just add postconditions to existing external methods.

Now I need to verify that the View trait is properly constrained — `TruncatedMemoryRegion<T>` only implements View when `T: Address + View<V = int>`, and since `PhysicalAddress` satisfies this constraint, the generic methods with `#[verus_spec]` should work correctly for that concrete type. The issue is that the trait bound on the impl block doesn't guarantee `View` is available, so Verus might reject the spec. Instead, I could define wrapper functions in frame.rs that are monomorphic and provide concrete specs for the specific types being used.

Writing wrapper functions... I'm checking what syntax Verus actually supports for assume_specification on generic types, but it looks like the documentation explicitly states that assume_specification can't match generic signatures. Looking at the existing examples in the codebase, they only work with non-generic types like FrameAddress. So I'm back to my original options: either add the specs directly to the region.rs methods, which means modifying that file, or find another approach.

For option three, I'm wondering if I can avoid needing these specs altogether. The issue is that without specs on start() and size(), I can't connect what those methods return to the region's properties in the verification. I could try adding ghost variables to track this information instead.

But that won't work either—I'd still need a way to connect the actual values returned by start() and size() to those ghost variables, which brings me back to needing specs. So I'm going to need specs on these methods after all.

Let me try option one first and see if it even compiles. I'm noticing that TruncatedMemoryRegion::start() is marked with external_body and just delegates to the underlying region's start() method.

The issue is that adding a spec with self@ requires View to be implemented for TruncatedMemoryRegion<T>, but that needs the bound T: Address + View<V=int>, which is stricter than the current impl bound of just T: Address. I'm not sure if Verus allows specs to add extra bounds like that.

Let me try a different approach—what if I create a separate impl block with the stricter bounds and add the spec there? Actually, that won't work since the function is already defined in the original impl block.

So I'm going to go with option 2 and create wrapper functions following the stdlib wrapper pattern. I'll define thin wrappers in frame.rs that can have the specs I need.

Writing wrapper functions... I need to understand how Verus handles the auto-deref chain here—when `region.start()` returns a `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>` and we call `into_frame_number()` on it, Verus needs to resolve through the `Deref` impl, but since that impl has `external_body` with no spec, Verus won't know the relationship between the wrapper and the underlying type. The key question is whether Verus can still apply the spec of `PhysicalAddress::into_frame_number` to the dereferenced value, and what view it assigns to that dereferenced `PhysicalAddress`.

Since the `Deref` impl lacks a spec, Verus can't establish that the auto-deref'd value has the view `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>@`, which breaks the proof chain. I'm considering a few approaches: adding a spec to the `Deref` impl itself, creating a wrapper that explicitly extracts and calls the method, or building a fully monomorphic wrapper that handles the entire chain in one go. I think the cleanest approach is option C—writing a wrapper function that encapsulates the full `region.start().into_frame_number().into_raw_value()` sequence with a single spec that directly relates the region's view to the final result.

I should apply the same pattern to other methods like `region.size()` that need specs, and then scan through the codebase to identify any other uses that might require similar wrappers. Looking more carefully at the loop structure, I see that line 284 actually calls `region.start().into_raw_value()` inside the error branch, and while the `error!` macro itself is cfg-gated, Verus still sees this line since it's not directly gated. So I need to handle the same deref issues here as well. I see there's already a wrapper function `pa_into_raw` in the frame address module that handles converting `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>` to a raw value with proper verification. Rather than calling `into_raw_value()` directly in the alloc_range code, I could use this existing helper instead, though that would require modifying the call sites. Let me think through what minimal changes are actually needed to get the proofs working.

I'm focusing on the critical path for the Inner methods. The simpler ones like `free` and `book` just need to handle frame number conversions and bitmap operations, while `alloc` and `alloc_range` are more involved with their loops and region handling. Let me start with the straightforward cases first.

Writing proof specifications... I'm verifying the postcondition for the success case: when the bitmap clears the frame number, I need to confirm that the old state contained this frame in its allocated set. Since frame_number is derived from frame@ by dividing by page_size, and the invariant guarantees frame@ is page-aligned, multiplying back gives us the original address, so the relationship holds. frame_number * ps from allocated, and the same logic applies to the free set. For the error case where the bitmap isn't covered or the bit isn't set, the state remains unchanged, so I need to verify the frame wasn't in allocated—which follows because either the bit wasn't set in the bitmap or the frame number wasn't in the covered range, both of which mean it can't be in the allocated set. This looks provable, but I need the conversion spec to formalize it.

I need the lemma that if `x % y == 0` and `y > 0`, then `(x / y) * y == x`, which is a standard arithmetic property that the SMT solver should handle directly. Let me now think through the concrete implementation and how the View interacts with set operations.

Now I'm working through how inserting a frame into the allocated_frames set relates to inserting the frame number into the underlying bitmap's set_bits. I need to show that these operations commute properly—that inserting `frame@` into the mapped set is equivalent to first inserting `frame_number` into `set_bits` and then applying the mapping.

The key insight is that this follows from the set-map-insert lemma, where the existential quantifier distributes over the disjunction created by the insert operation. The SMT solver might handle this automatically, but I may need to provide an explicit lemma if it doesn't.

Now I'm ready to start implementing. I'll begin by marking the public wrapper functions as external_body to match the free specification, then add the conversion specs to frame.spec.rs, and finally define the concrete View in frame.proof.

## Blocking Issues

1. **`FrameNumber::from_raw_value` “always succeeds” is not justified by the current module invariant**
   - **Impact:** This makes the spec stronger than the implementation. `Inner::alloc()` really can hit the `None` branch if `bitmap.alloc()` returns an index above `FrameNumber::MAX` (`frame.rs:94-119`, `number.rs:47-53`).
   - **Severity:** Blocking
   - **Fix:** Don’t totalize `FrameNumber::from_raw_value` unless you first strengthen `Inner::internal_inv()` to prove every covered bitmap index is a valid frame number. Otherwise keep the error path real and either:
     - roll back `bitmap.alloc()` on conversion failure, or
     - weaken the `alloc` spec accordingly.

2. **`internal_inv() == bitmap.inv()` is too weak for this module**
   - **Impact:** It does not connect bitmap indices to valid frame addresses. Your proposed `View` can contain abstract frame addresses that no runtime `FrameAddress` can represent/prove. This also blocks the “conversion failure is unreachable” argument.
   - **Severity:** Blocking
   - **Fix:** Add a real allocator invariant, e.g. every covered index:
     - is nonnegative,
     - maps to a page-aligned address,
     - and yields a representable frame/address (`i * spec_page_size() <= usize::MAX`, or equivalent frame-number bound).
     Then prove `self@.wf()` from `bitmap.inv() + internal_inv()`.

3. **`alloc_range` still needs a coverage guarantee, especially under the cfg-gated fast path**
   - **Impact:** `SparseBitmap::test()` returns `Ok(false)` for uncovered indices. So if the pre-validation loop is absent, the free-check loop can pass, and the set-all loop can partially mutate before failing (`frame.rs:256-306`). That contradicts `Err(_) => self@ == old(self)@`.
   - **Severity:** Blocking
   - **Fix:** Your plan needs an explicit proof assumption/invariant that every frame in the region is covered by the bitmap, or the code/spec must change. This is not solved by the conversion specs alone.

4. **`TruncatedMemoryRegion` wrappers as proposed are too weak**
   - **Impact:** `alloc_range` uses `start_frame_number + num_frames`; your current region spec only gives alignment (`region.rs:481-485`), not the constructor’s non-overflow/end-in-range fact (`region.rs:151-178`). You’ll likely get stuck proving arithmetic safety and range correspondence.
   - **Severity:** Blocking
   - **Fix:** Expose stronger facts, preferably at the source methods, not just local wrappers:
     - `start() == self@.start`
     - `size() == self@.size`
     - enough bound info to derive the frame-number range safely.

## Non-Blocking Issues

1. **Leaving `FrameNumber::into_raw_value` empty will hurt downstream proofs**
   - **Impact:** Even if not unsound, it leaves you with an opaque getter right where you need arithmetic equalities.
   - **Severity:** Non-Blocking
   - **Fix:** Give it a usable spec, or define a `View` for `FrameNumber`.

2. **The `View` shape is conceptually right, but proof-hostile as written**
   - **Impact:** Repeated existential comprehensions will make lemmas noisy.
   - **Severity:** Non-Blocking
   - **Fix:** Introduce a helper like `frame_addr_of(i)` / `frames_of(indices)` and prove mapping lemmas once.

## Suggestions

1. **Step 1 looks fine**
   - External-body wrappers for singleton free functions are a reasonable workaround.

2. **Q1 answer**
   - `FrameAddress::from_frame_number` being total is reasonable.
   - `FrameNumber::from_raw_value` being total is **not** reasonable without a stronger allocator invariant.

3. **Q2 answer**
   - Your concrete `View` is the right abstraction, **provided** you strengthen `internal_inv()`.

4. **Q4 answer**
   - Wrapper approach is acceptable, but adding specs directly to `TruncatedMemoryRegion::start/size` would be cleaner and more reusable if Verus accepts it.

5. **Q5 loop invariants**
   - **Coverage loop:** `self == old(self)` and all indices in `[start, index)` are covered.
   - **Free-check loop:** `self == old(self)` and all indices in `[start, index)` are covered and not set.
   - **Set-all loop:** let `prefix = [start, index)`. Invariant should say `self@ == old(self)@.spec_alloc_range(prefix_frames)` and `prefix_frames ⊆ old(self)@.free_frames`.

Overall: **good direction, but the plan currently papers over the hardest issue: the missing invariant that bitmap indices correspond to valid, covered frame addresses.** Without that, Step 2 is too strong and `alloc_range`’s error spec is still exposed.

[THINKING] Now I have a clear picture. Let me plan the proofs:

### Inner::free proof strategy
```
free(&mut self, frame: FrameAddress) -> Result<(), Error>
```
- **Code**: `frame_addr_to_bitmap_index(frame)` → `bitmap.clear(frame_number)`
- **Ensures (Ok)**: old(self)@.allocated_frames.contains(frame@) ∧ self@ == old(self)@.spec_free(frame@)
- **Ensures (Err)**: self@ == old(self)@ ∧ !old(self)@.allocated_frames.contains(frame@)


- **From wrappers**: `frame_addr_to_bitmap_index` converts the frame address to a bitmap index by dividing by the page size
- **From bitmap.clear**: On success, the index must be covered and set, then gets removed from the bit set; on failure, the state remains unchanged and the index wasn't set to begin with
- **Connecting the pieces**: When clear succeeds, the frame was allocated and the state transitions to spec_free; when it fails, nothing changes and the frame wasn't allocated

.
- **The view transformation**: After clearing the bitmap at index, the new allocated_frames set should reflect removing that frame from the old set, while free_frames gains it back.
- **The proof obligation**: I need to show these two descriptions are equivalent — that the bitmap operation (removing index from set_bits) produces the same frame set as the spec_free operation (removing frame@ from allocated_frames).
- **The key insight**: For any address, it's in the new allocated set if and only if there exists some index in the cleared bitmap pointing to it, which should match the spec_free condition where the address exists in the old set but isn't the freed frame.
- **Case analysis**: When the address equals the freed frame (index * ps), both sides correctly evaluate to false since no other index can map to the same address. When the address differs from the freed frame, the conditions become equivalent because any index mapping to that address must be different from the freed index, so it remains in the cleared bitmap.

- **Key insight**: The injectivity of frame_addr_of when ps > 0 is crucial for this equivalence to hold.

- **Proof strategy**: Rather than proving each method independently, I should structure the proof around a core lemma that establishes how bitmap removal corresponds to the spec's allocated set removal. Each method then follows the same pattern: relate bitmap indices to frame addresses, apply the bitmap postconditions, and show the new state matches the spec transition.

- **Critical sub-lemma**: I need to prove that removing an element from the domain before mapping is equivalent to mapping first then removing from the codomain. This requires showing the equivalence holds for every address, including the boundary case where addr equals the removed element's mapped value.

- **For addr == k*ps**: Both sides evaluate to false—the left side has no witness since k isn't in the reduced domain, and the right side removes exactly this address.

- **For addr != k*ps**: Both sides reduce to checking whether the address exists in the original set, since any witness must have i ≠ k anyway.

Now I'm seeing that I should build general-purpose lemmas connecting bitmap state transitions to view state changes, then each method proof becomes straightforward: apply wrapper specs, apply bitmap specs, then invoke the connecting lemma. Though writing this out in Verus will be quite verbose. Let me explore whether I can leverage set extensionality more directly to simplify the proof structure.

I can use extensional equality to assert that two sets are the same by showing they contain identical elements. Since loops 1 and 2 don't modify anything, the state at loop 3's start is still the original state, so I can use `old(self)` to reference the initial bitmap and chunks before any modifications happen in loop 3.

Now I need to establish that after loop 3 completes (when `index == end`), the final `set_bits` equals the original `set_bits` plus the range `[start, end)`. Then I can show the overall postcondition: that `self` matches the spec view where allocated frames are added and freed frames are removed from the original state.

For the allocated set, I'm verifying that mapping the new set bits through the frame-to-address operation gives me the old allocated frames plus the newly allocated ones. For the free set, I need to show that removing the newly allocated frames from the old free set gives me the correct result by applying the same set theory reasoning.

The key insight is that when a function is injective, removing mapped sets distributes over the difference operation, so the math checks out. However, formalizing all these proofs in Verus is going to require substantial work.

I'm identifying the core lemmas I'll need to establish: proving that frame_addr_of is injective, showing how mapping and removal interact, and handling the relationship between set differences and insertions/removals in various combinations.

I also need to connect the bitmap's is_full() state to whether free_frames is empty, then start implementing the simplest function first before reconsidering if there's a more tractable proof approach for this complex problem.

Actually, I'm going to step back and try a different strategy—instead of building general lemmas, I'll have each method proof establish set extensionality directly within itself, which keeps things self-contained even if it means some duplication.

For the `free` function, I'm setting up the proof by converting the frame address to a bitmap index and establishing the key relationship that the frame value equals the index times the page size.

[TOOL] task: {'name': 'plan-critique', 'agent_type': 'rubber-duck', 'description': 'Validate 

[THINKING] `lemma_fundamental_div_mod(x, d)` ensures `x == d * (x / d) + (x % d)`.

So `lemma_fundamental_div_mod(frame@, ps)` gives `frame@ == ps * (frame@ / ps) + frame@ % ps`.

Since `frame@ % ps == 0`, this becomes `frame@ == ps * (frame@ / ps) = ps * frame_number`.
And `ps * frame_number == frame_number * ps` by commutativity.
So `frame@ == frame_number * ps == frame_addr_of(frame_number)`.

Mostly sound, but there are 3 real blind spots.

## Blocking Issues

1. **Step 1 is too weak / potentially unsound**
   - **Issue:** `index * ps <= usize::MAX` is **not enough** to justify `bitmap_index_to_frame_addr(index).is_ok()`. `FrameNumber::from_raw_value()` fails when `index > FrameNumber::MAX`, and `FrameNumber::MAX = MAX_ADDRESS / FRAME_SIZE - 1` (`src/libs/arch/src/x86/mem/paging/frame/number.rs:27-53`).  
   - **Impact:** You could prove `alloc()` from a false trusted spec.
   - **Fix:** Don’t strengthen to unconditional `ret.is_ok()` from the multiplication bound alone. Require/prove a bound equivalent to `index <= FrameNumber::MAX`, or derive it from a stronger allocator invariant. Also note: `frame.rs` already has unconditional `ret.is_ok()` on this wrapper (`src/kernel/src/mm/phys/frame.rs:74-83`), so audit that first.

2. **`alloc_range` loop 3 needs a stronger invariant than your sketch**
   - **Issue:** The function spec says on `Err`, `self@ == old(self)@` (`frame.rs:304-307`), but loop 3 mutates before the `Err` branch (`frame.rs:367-375`). So the `set()` failure must be proved unreachable. Your current invariant sketch doesn’t preserve enough to show the *current* index is still covered and unset.
   - **Impact:** Proof will stall exactly at the rollback/error path.
   - **Fix:** Carry both:
     - `self.bitmap@.chunks =~= old(self).bitmap@.chunks`
     - `self.bitmap@.set_bits =~= old(self).bitmap@.set_bits ∪ set_int_range(start, index)`
     - plus a pre-loop fact `forall j in [start,end): old(self).bitmap@.is_covered(j) && !old(self).bitmap@.is_bit_set(j)`
     Then `set(index)` success follows immediately.

3. **The cfg-gated coverage loop creates a config-dependent proof obligation**
   - **Issue:** `SparseBitmap::test()` returns `Ok(false)` for uncovered indices (`src/libs/sparse-bitmap/src/lib.rs:245-257`). So loop 2 alone cannot distinguish “free” from “uncovered”. The extra `find_chunk()` pass is what makes loop 3 safe (`frame.rs:324-340`).
   - **Impact:** If `nightly-performance-optimizations` is enabled, your proof story changes materially; the current spec may no longer match behavior.
   - **Fix:** Either:
     - prove only under the verified cfg and state that explicitly, or
     - strengthen caller assumptions/spec so uncovered indices are impossible without loop 1.

## Non-Blocking Issues

1. **`TruncatedMemoryRegion::inv()` exposes too little for the arithmetic you need**
   - **Issue:** Its spec only gives page alignment (`region.rs:481-485`), but your proof also wants safe reasoning about `start + size` and `start_frame + num_frames` (`frame.rs:317-322`, `295-297`).
   - **Impact:** You may hit arithmetic proof friction later.
   - **Fix:** Add/use a lemma or wrapper spec exposing the constructor fact that the region range is representable (`region.rs:163-178`).

## Suggestions

1. **Use lemmas, not inline extensionality everywhere**
   - Best 3 reusable lemmas:
     - `frame_addr_of` injective on nonnegative indices with `spec_page_size() > 0`
     - bitmap `set_bits.insert/remove(i)` lifts to `allocated/free` insert/remove of `frame_addr_of(i)`
     - bitmap extensional equality (`bitmap@ =~= old(bitmap)@`) implies `Inner` view equality
   - This will reduce repetition across `free`, `book`, and `alloc`.

2. **`alloc()` Err case needs one bridge lemma**
   - From `bitmap.is_full()` to `old(self)@.free_frames.is_empty()`.
   - Don’t leave that as ad hoc set reasoning.

## Direct Answers

1. **Is the plan sound?**  
   **Mostly, but not fully.** The biggest miss is Step 1: your proposed wrapper strengthening is not justified as stated.

2. **Separate lemmas or inline?**  
   **Separate lemmas.** This is exactly the kind of repeated quantified reasoning that becomes unmaintainable inline.

3. **Is `∀j∈[index,end). covered(j) && !bit_set(j)` enough for loop 3?**  
   **Not by itself.** You also need a prefix-update invariant (what has already been set) and chunk-shape preservation.

4. **Does Verus see the cfg-gated loop?**  
   **Yes under the default verify config.** `make verify` uses `--no-default-features --features $(MACHINE),$(LOG_LEVEL)` with defaults `MACHINE=microvm`, `LOG_LEVEL=trace` (`Makefile:14,45,313-345`). `microvm,trace` does **not** include `nightly-performance-optimizations` (`src/kernel/Cargo.toml:46-95`), so the `#[cfg(not(feature = "nightly-performance-optimizations"))]` loop is included in verification.
[RESULT ✓] task: Mostly sound, but there are 3 real blind spots.

   - **Issue:** `index * ps <= usize::MAX` is **not enough** to justify `bitmap_index_to_frame_addr(index).is_ok()`. `FrameNumber::from_raw_value()` fails when `index > FrameNumber::M...

[THINKING] `alloc_range` is the most complex function. Let me plan the proof carefully.

The spec uses:- `start_frame_number = region@.start / spec_page_size()`- `end_frame_number = (region@.start + region@.size) / spec_page_size()`- `frame_numbers = set_int_range(start_frame_number, end_frame_number)`
- `frames = frame_numbers.map(|i: int| i * spec_page_size())`The exec code computes:- `start_frame_number = region_start_frame_number(region)` → `region@.start / ps`


- `num_frames = region_size_raw(region) / mem::FRAME_SIZE` → `region@.size / ps`
- `end_frame_number = start_frame_number + num_frames` → `region@.start / ps + region@.size / ps`

The key insight is showing that `start_frame_number + num_frames` equals `(region@.start + region@.size) / ps`. Since both `region@.start` and `region@.size` are divisible by the page size from the region invariant, I can use the property that `(a + b) / ps = a/ps + b/ps` when both operands are divisible by `ps`.

I need to formalize this as a lemma: when `a` and `b` are both divisible by `d` and `d > 0`, then `(a+b)/d == a/d + b/d`. This follows from the fundamental division-modulo theorem since we can express `a` and `b` as multiples of `d`, making their sum also a multiple of `d`.

Overall: **plan is viable**, but I see **1 blocking proof gap** and a few important adjustments.

## Blocking Issues

1. **Loop 3 invariant loses old-state information**
   - **Impact:** Your current loop-3 invariant is enough to describe the **current** bitmap, but not enough to finish the **spec**, especially:
     - `frames.subset_of(old(self)@.free_frames)`
     - proving `set(index)` cannot fail on the success path
   - **Why:** After the first `set`, the current state no longer says the whole target range was free. You need that fact from the **pre-loop state**.
   - **Fix:** Carry a ghost snapshot (e.g. `ghost old_bm = old(self).bitmap@`) and add an invariant like:
     - `self.bitmap@.chunks =~= old_bm.chunks`
     - `forall j: int. sfn <= j < efn ==> old_bm.is_covered(j) && !old_bm.set_bits.contains(j)`
     - plus your processed-range equation for current `set_bits`
   - Then derive the “future indices still free in current state” fact from:
     - `self.bitmap@.set_bits =~= old_bm.set_bits ∪ set_int_range(sfn, index)`
     - disjointness of `[sfn,index)` and `[index,efn)`.

## Non-Blocking Issues

2. **Do not `admit()` the `bitmap.test()` Err branch**
   - **Impact:** That weakens the proof and violates the usual guardrails.
   - **Evidence:** `SparseBitmap::test` ensures `result is Ok` (`src/libs/sparse-bitmap/src/lib.rs:248-255`).
   - **Fix:** Bind the result first, prove `r is Ok`, then eliminate the `Err` arm as unreachable. Use `assert(false)`/unreachable-style proof, not `admit`.

3. **Loop 1 is genuinely needed under the current contracts**
   - **Impact:** Without it, loop 2 can accept uncovered indices because `test(index)` returns `Ok(false)` for uncovered bits (`src/libs/sparse-bitmap/src/lib.rs:245-269`), while `set(index)` later fails when `!is_covered || is_bit_set` (`src/libs/sparse-bitmap/src/lib.rs:1619-1635`).
   - **Fix:** Keep loop 1 in the verified config, and use `find_chunk(index).is_some() <==> is_covered(index)` (`src/libs/sparse-bitmap/src/lib.rs:411-416`) to establish the coverage fact you carry into loop 2/3.

## Suggestions

4. **Q1: Is loop 3 invariant sufficient?**
   - **Answer:** **Not by itself.**
   - It is sufficient for the **current-state** shape, but not for the final spec unless you also preserve the **old-range-free/covered** fact across loop 3.

5. **Q3: How to handle `Err` from `bitmap.test()`?**
   - **Answer:** **Prove unreachable**, don’t `admit`.

6. **Q4: Is `Set::map` distributive over union?**
   - **Answer:** **Yes.**
   - `vstd::set_lib::lemma_map_union_commute` exists (`vstd set_lib.rs:511-514`).

7. **Q5: Map over difference**
   - **Answer:** I would **not** look for a generic lemma first.
   - **Better fix:** prove `free_frames` extensionally from the view definition in `frame.proof.rs:20-25`, using:
     - `new_set_bits = old_set_bits ∪ range`
     - `chunks` unchanged
     - injectivity of `frame_addr_of(i) = i * spec_page_size()` from `spec_page_size() > 0` (same idea already used in `frame.proof.rs:68-78`).
   - This is usually easier than forcing a generic `map(difference)` lemma.

## Direct answers

1. **Loop 3 invariant sufficient?**  
   **No**, unless you also carry the old-state “entire range covered and free” fact.

2. **Need cfg-gated loop 1?**  
   **Yes**, under current `test`/`set` specs.

3. **`bitmap.test()` Err branch?**  
   **Prove unreachable**; do not `admit`.

4. **`Set::map` over union?**  
   **Yes** — use `lemma_map_union_commute`.

5. **Lemma for free-frame difference?**  
   I don’t see an obvious ready-made one worth depending on; **direct extensional proof is the safer path**.

So: **no architectural blocker**, but **carry old-state facts through loop 3** or the proof likely stalls near the final postcondition.

## Plan

### Pre-loop arithmetic setup
1. Establish `start_frame_number as int == region@.start / spec_page_size()`
2. Establish `num_frames as int == region@.size / spec_page_size()` (since `region_size_raw / FRAME_SIZE` and `FRAME_SIZE == spec_page_size()`)
3. Establish `end_frame_number as int == (region@.start + region@.size) / spec_page_size()`
   - This requires proving: `(a + b) / d == a/d + b/d` when `a % d == 0 && b % d == 0 && d > 0`
   - I can apply `lemma_fundamental_div_mod` twice to show `a == d*(a/d)` and `b == d*(b/d)`, then combine them to get `a + b == d*(a/d + b/d)`, which gives us `(a+b)/d == a/d + b/d`

### Define ghost constants and set up loop invariants
I'm introducing ghost variables for the page size and frame numbers, then establishing the loop invariant that tracks bitmap validity and coverage of the frame range.

### Analyzing the error path
If the loop exits early because a frame isn't covered, that frame's address won't be in the free_frames set (since coverage is required), but it will be in the frames set derived from the frame number range, which means the frames set isn't a subset of free_frames as required.

I also need to verify that the bitmap state hasn't changed, which follows directly from the invariant that keeps it equivalent to the original.

### Loop 2 invariant (free check)
Continuing to establish the invariant for the second loop...

After the first loop succeeds, I know every frame number in the range is covered. Now in the second loop, I'm checking which bits are actually set in the bitmap. The challenge is that calling `test` requires the full `self.inv()` condition, which includes both the bitmap invariant and the internal invariant. I have the internal invariant from the loop invariant, but I need to verify the bitmap invariant holds to make the test call valid.

When `test` returns true, the frame is allocated—the bit is set, which means that frame address is definitely not in the free frames list (since free frames only contains unset bits). So the frame must be in the allocated frames, contradicting the assumption that all frames are free. In the error path, `test` always succeeds according to the spec, so that branch is unreachable.

The second fact combined with the fact that we're only adding indices less than the current one means the current index can't already be set.

But I'm wondering if there's a different approach altogether. The real problem is that the exec code does `let end_frame_number: usize = start_frame_number + num_frames;` without an overflow check, and Verus will reject this unless I can prove the addition won't overflow. The `internal_inv` tells me that covered frame indices stay within bounds, but I don't have a guarantee that the range [start_frame_number, end_frame_number) is actually covered.

The overflow could happen before the loop even validates the range, which looks like a genuine bug in the original code. Since the user's instructions say to auto-fix overflow issues, I should use a checked addition here and handle the overflow case properly. `checked_add` or `saturating_add` and return an error if it overflows. Or, we could strengthen the FRAME_SIZE spec to include `result >= 2`. Let me think about which is cleaner.

Option A is more robust as it handles the actual case with checked addition, but it modifies exec code. The error path would need to prove that when the sum overflows, the frames set contains some element not in free_frames — since all frame addresses in free_frames are bounded by `usize::MAX`, any overflow would create a frame address exceeding that bound, which wouldn't be in free_frames.

Option B is simpler: if we require `FRAME_SIZE >= 2`, then both `start/ps` and `size/ps` are at most `usize::MAX/2`, so their sum can't overflow. This avoids the complex proof entirely.

Now I need to verify this works in Verus by checking that `region@.start` is actually bounded by `usize::MAX` — it should be since it's a usize value, but let me confirm that's guaranteed. It comes from `self.start@` where `self.start` is `PageAligned<PhysicalAddress>`. `PageAligned<T>` has `inner: T`, and `PhysicalAddress` wraps a usize. So `self.start@ <= usize::MAX`. Yes.

For loops 1 and 2, the Err returns definitely require `self.lemma_inv_implies_wf()`, and I already have `internal_inv` in both of those invariants, so that's fine.

The real problem is I need to add variable equalities to track the relationship between `start_frame_number`, `end_frame_number`, and the region bounds. I should add constraints like `start_frame_number as int == region@.start / spec_page_size()` to each loop invariant—these should be trivially maintained since all the values involved are immutable. Though I'm realizing the invariant is a predicate, so I need to think about how to properly express this.

Now I'm working through whether these equalities are actually available at each loop entry. The facts I proved before loop 1 should carry through to establish the invariant at loop 1 entry, but I need to verify that the immutable variable relationships persist after each loop exits. a loop exits, the verification context is:
1. The negated loop guard
2. The invariant (with the loop variable at its final value)
3. Facts from the enclosing scope that were available before the loop (about immutable things)

Wait, I'm confused about Verus's loop semantics. Let me look at this more carefully.

In standard verification:
- Before loop: context C
- At loop entry: check invariant INV(init) under C
- Inside loop body: assume INV(k), prove INV(k+1)
- After loop: assume INV(final) && !guard

Facts from C are NOT available inside the loop body (only INV is assumed). But facts from C ARE available after the loop exits (they're part of the surrounding context).

So between loops 1 and 2 (and between 2 and 3), the fact `end_frame_number as int == (region@.start + region@.size) / spec_page_size()` IS available since it's in the surrounding context C. It's only inside each loop body that it's lost.

I need to add this fact to the invariant of each loop so I can use it inside the loop body. The invariants are already quite long, so I'll be minimal and add the frame number calculations to each loop invariant, particularly at the Err paths for loops 1 and 2.
